def beta_obj(u):
    e = np.exp(u - u.max()); a=e/e.sum()
    c = convolve(a,a) maybe normalized
    maxc = c.max()
    # use shifted logsumexp: t = beta*maxc; logZ = log(sum(exp(beta*c - t))) + t
    vals = beta*(c - maxc); exps = np.exp(vals); s=np.sum(exps)

    return Mbeta, grad? For L-BFGS, define returns f, g.

    else fft.
    grad_u = g - a * g.sum()  # because dM/da * (I - 1 a^T)

```
def square_convolve(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n < 2048:
        return np.convolve(a,a)
    N=1
    size=2*n-1
    while N<size: N*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,n=N)
    ca=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,n=N)[:size]
    return ca
```
Accuracy okay double. For small, direct avoids FFT round-off.
- `cross_convolve_shift(p, a)` need h_i = Σ_j a_j p_{i+j}. For direct if n<2048: `np.convolve(p, a[::-1], 'full')[n-1:n-1+n]`. For FFT:

```

```
def norm_nonneg(x, max_val=1000.0):
   x=np.asarray(x,float).reshape(-1)
   x=np.clip(x,0,max_val)
   s=x.sum()
   if s < 1e-12: return np.ones(len(x) or 1)/...
   return x/s
```

```
def safe_eval(seq):
   try: val=evaluate_sequence(seq.tolist())
   except: val=np.inf
   return val
```
(No closures.)

def add_candidate(cands, seq):

        score=safe_eval(arr) # re-evaluate maybe same
        # remove dominated same length? not necessary

    return np.inf
```

```
def square_convolve_auto(a, support=None, dense_threshold=20000):
    n=len(a)
    if support is None: support=np.nonzero(a>0)[0]
    if len(support)*len(support) < 2*dense_threshold? maybe if len(support)<2000:
       c=np.zeros(2*n-1)
       for idx,i in enumerate(support):
          ai=a[i]
          # i+support, add ai*a[j]*factor
          sums = i+support
          vals = ai*a[support]
          # add twice for pairs i<j, once for i==j. Simpler double then adjust diagonal
          np.add.at(c, sums, vals*2)
       diag = 2*support
       c[2*support] -= a[support]**2  # because added twice
       return c
    else: full convolution
```

```
def try_sequence(seq, best, tag=""):
   arr = norm_nonneg(seq)
   if arr.size==0: return np.inf
   score = safe_eval(arr.tolist())
   if np.isfinite(score) and score < best[0]:
       best[0] = score
       best[1] = arr.copy()
       if verbose: print(...)
   return score
```

FFT square:
```
def convolve_square_fft(a):
    n = a.size
    size=2*n-1
    N=1
    while N<size: N <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:size]
    # Remove tiny negative due to roundoff? max uses abs? Convolution should be nonnegative; clip c = np.maximum(c,0)
    return np.maximum(c,0)
```
Clipping can slightly affect gradient? For objective only. For gradient, p uses clipped c.
For cross_dot FFT:
```
def cross_dot_pdf(p,a):
    n=a.size
    size=len(p)+n-1
    N=1...
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fp*fa,N)
    return conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
For direct threshold <=512 maybe.

Potential roundoff makes c negative; use `np.where(c>0,c,0)`.

   c=square_convolve_auto(a)

Top-level `support_from_greedy(max_len, R, strategy, rng)` returns array.

Detailed code for greedy:
```
def greedy_b2g(max_len, R, strategy='min', rng=None):
    if max_len < 2: return np.zeros(0)
    L=max_len
    S=[0]
    counts = np.zeros(2*L-1, dtype=np.int64)
    counts[0]=1
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng()
    for _ in range(10000):
       if len(S) >= L: break
       last=S[-1]
       start=last+1
       if start >= L: break
       xrange = np.arange(start, L, dtype=np.int64)
       # candidate max
       base = counts.max()
       cand = np.full(xrange.shape, base, dtype=np.int64)
       for s in S:
           vals = counts[s + xrange] + 2
           # only elements where s+xrange < len counts? max s+L-1 ≤ L-1+L-1=2L-2 ok
           cand = np.maximum(cand, vals)
       diag = 2*xrange
       # diag positions <= 2L-2? x≤L-1 =>2L-2 valid
       diag_vals = counts[diag] + 1
       cand = np.maximum(cand, diag_vals)
       feas = cand <= R
       if not np.any(feas): break
       idxs = np.nonzero(feas)[0]
       if strategy == 'min': idx = idxs[0]
       elif strategy == 'max': idx=idxs[-1]
       elif strategy == 'rand':
           # weight by slack
           w = (R+1 - cand[idxs]).astype(float)
           p = w / w.sum()
           idx = idxs[rng.choice(len(idxs), p=p)]
       else: # balance
           # min cand, then maybe min x
           bestc = cand[idxs].min()
           min_idxs = idxs[cand[idxs]==bestc]
           # choose median x
           idx = min_idxs[len(min_idxs)//2]
       x = int(xrange[idx])
       for s in S:
           counts[x+s] += 2
       counts[2*x] += 1
       S.append(x)
       if 2*x >= 2*L-1: break
    S=np.array(S, dtype=int)
    n = S.max()+1 if S.size else 0
    if n == 0: return np.zeros(1)
    vec=np.zeros(n); vec[S]=1.0/len(S)
    return vec
```
Note base = counts.max() before adding; but if updated positions have lower than base, base already included. Good. For diag, if diag position same as x+s? No s=x not. But what if x+0 = x and diag=2x; distinct for x>0. Good.
Potential overflow of int64? counts up to R small, fine.
This greedy enforces counts≤R for **all** lags, but does not consider that after adding x, existing pairs counts unchanged. Invariant ensures ≤R.
We should ensure positions `counts[s+xrange]` when s+start maybe >2L-2? max s L-1, x max L-1 =>2L-2, okay.
Strategy 'max' choosing largest feasible may create sparse long support with score maybe worse but might allow LP find good weights. Keep.

Could there be a feasible x with x+S exceeding 2L-1 when x near L-1 and s near L-1? max 2L-2, length 2L-1 indices 0..2L-2 ok. Good.

After support vector length n=last+1, the counts array length 2L-1 maybe larger than 2n-1 if L>last+1. But all sums of S are within 2n-2; counts beyond 2n-2 zero because last< L? If max S < L-1, positions above 2max+? zero. For score, n=last+1. If we generated in max_len L but actual last smaller, counts length larger; no issue. But we passed max_len L for generating; the sequence vector length n=last+1. The constraints for B2g were with interval 0..L-1; but if we truncate vector to actual length, that's the same support; valid. The span is smaller than L, score potentially better. If generator stopped because no feasible x<L, actual last might be far less than L-1? It stops if next x start> L? It only breaks when start>=L (last=L-1) or no feasible in [last+1,L-1]. Let's design `smooth_anneal` more concretely.

Top-level `softmax_fast(u)`:
```
z=u-u[-1]? max; e=np.exp(z); return e/e.sum()
```

Top-level `beta_objective(u, beta)`:
- n = u.size
- a=softmax
- c=square_convolve_dense_auto(a) (dense)
- maxc = c.max()
- shifted = beta*(c - maxc)
- exps = np.exp(shifted)
- sumexp = exps.sum()
- Mbeta = maxc + np.log(sumexp)/beta
- p = exps / sumexp
- h = cross_dot_dense_auto(p,a)
- g = 2*h
- grad = g - a*g.sum()
- return float(Mbeta), grad
Need if sumexp=0? no.
Potential if beta large and c has many identical, exps stable.

But if beta=0? We use >0.

In smooth_anneal, after each beta, we can also perform a *re-weighting* exact active-set: set tiny weights to zero, maybe add random small noise to escape. We can use `rng.random` to choose.

```
def smooth_anneal(a_init, time_limit, rng, best):
    a = norm_nonneg(a_init)
    if a.size < 4 or time_limit <=0: return a
    n=a.size
    c0 = square_convolve_dense_auto(a)
    M0 = c0.max()
    score0 = 2*n*M0
    if not finite: return a
    u = np.log(a + 1e-15) - np.max(np.log(a + 1e-15))
    beta = max(1.0, 5.0 / max(M0, 1e-12))
    max_it = 30 if n > 800 else 80
    start=time.monotonic()
    for _ in range(4):
       if time.monotonic() - start > time_limit: break
       # limit individual minimize time roughly: choose maxiter based on remaining
       res = minimize(beta_objective, u, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', args=(beta,), options={'maxiter': max_it, 'maxls':50, 'ftol':1e-10, 'gtol':1e-6})
       if res.x is not None: u = np.asarray(res.x)
       a = softmax_fast(u)
       # zero tiny? for sparse maybe if max a >1e-6 and min < 1e-12
       a[a < 1e-14] = 0.0
       a = norm_nonneg(a)
       score = safe_eval(a.tolist()) # maybe expensive; use internal? but safe few.
       if finite and score < best[0]: update
       beta *= 4
       if time.monotonic() - start > time_limit: break
       # optionally random kick: if beta objective not decreasing?
    return a
```

def lp_direction(f, lp_time=5.0, max_nonzeros=4000000):
   n=f.size; sup = np.nonzero(f > 1e-12)[0]
   if sup.size==0: return f
   if sup.size * n > max_nonzeros: return None
   c = square_convolve_support(f, sup)  # full length maybe; M=c.max()
   if M<=0: return f
   L=2*n-1
   rows_list=[]; cols_list=[]; data_list=[]
   cols=np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
   for i in sup:
       rows = i + cols
       # rows length n; max <=2n-2
       rows_list.append(rows)
       cols_list.append(cols.copy())
       data_list.append(np.full(n, f[i], dtype=np.float64))
   rows = np.concatenate(rows_list); cols=...; data=...
   B=coo_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(L,n)).tocsr()
   c_obj=-np.ones(n)
   b_ub=M*np.ones(L)
   ub = max(10.0, 1e6) # maybe
   res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=B, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=[(0,ub)]*n, method='highs', options={'time_limit': lp_time, 'disp': False})
   if res.status not in (0,3,8?) and no x: return None
   g = np.asarray(res.x)
   if not finite or sum<=0: return None
   g=np.clip(g,0,None); g/=g.sum()
   return g
```
HiGHS status codes: 0 optimal, 1 pruned? 2 infeasible, 3 iteration limit? 4 model limit? 5? 8? We can use `if res.x is not None and np.isfinite(res.x).all() and res.x.sum()>0`. `res.status` maybe not needed.
Time limit options: For `method='highs'`, options key `time_limit` yes. Also `dual_feasibility`, etc not.

`square_convolve_support(f, sup)` for M:
```
def square_convolve_support(a, sup):
    n=a.size
    L=2*n-1
    c=np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    for i in sup:
        ai=a[i]
        if ai ==0: continue
        c[i+sup] += 2*ai*a[sup]
    c[2*sup] -= a[sup]**2
    return np.maximum(c,0)
```
Loop in Python over sup; if sup m=500, vector ops length500, okay. But `i+sup` allocated each loop. Could do double sum matrix? Not. Use this.

Can `i+sup` exceed L? max 2n-2 ok.

LP constraints b length 2n-1; if n large 5000, b 9999, fine. Building sparse B with nonzeros m*n; if m=100,n=5000=500k, ok. LP with 5000 vars, 10000 constraints, 500k nnz, time maybe seconds. Use small time.

Implementation details:

`_norm(a)`:
```
def _norm(a):
    x = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if x.size == 0: return np.ones(1)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return np.ones(x.size) / x.size
    return x / s
```
But zeroing tiny after norm; maybe:
```
x[x < 1e-15 * max(1.0, x.max())] = 0; re-sum
```
No need.

`_safe_score(seq)`:
```
def _safe_score(seq):
    try:
       val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq])
    except Exception:
       return np.inf
    return float(val) if np.isfinite(val) else np.inf
```
Note if seq is np array; convert list. The evaluator double clips; no issue.

`_try_seq`:
```
def _try_seq(seq, best, tag=""):
   arr = _norm(seq)
   if arr.size==0 or not np.isfinite(arr).all(): return np.inf
   # optional zero tiny
   arr[arr < 1e-14 * max(1.0, float(arr.max()))] = 0.0
   s = arr.sum()
   if s < 0.01: ...
   arr = arr / s
   score = _safe_score(arr)
   if np.isfinite(score) and score < best[0]:
      best[0]=score; best[1]=arr.copy()
      print(f"[{time.monotonic():.1f}] {tag} score={score:.6f} len={arr.size} support={np.count_nonzero(arr)}")
   return score
```

def _internal_score(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1)
   if a.size==0: return inf
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000); s=a.sum()
   if s<0.01: return inf
   c=_conv_square(a)
   m = float(np.max(c))
   if not finite: return inf
   return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```

`_conv_square(a)`: decide dense vs sparse based on support count threshold:
```
def _conv_square(a):
    n=a.size
    size=2*n-1
    sup = np.nonzero(a > 1e-15 * max(1.0, a.max()))[0] if n>0 else np.array([],int)
    if sup.size == 0: return np.zeros(size)
    if sup.size <= 1500 and sup.size * sup.size <= 4_000_000:
       c=np.zeros(size)
       for i in sup:
          ai=a[i]
          c[i+sup] += 2*ai*a[sup]
       c[2*sup] -= a[sup]**2
       c[np.abs(c)<1e-18]=0? maybe clip negative
       return np.maximum(c,0)
    if n <= 2048:
       return np.maximum(np.convolve(a,a),0)
    N=1; while N<size: N<<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:size]; return np.maximum(c,0)
```
But if a dense and sup.size ~n=2000, sup.size^2=4m, okay; use sparse loop maybe 2000*2000=4m with 2000 numpy additions length2000 ->4m operations but Python loop overhead 2000, okay. For n=5000 dense sup.size 5000, sup^2 25m > threshold, use FFT. Good.
If a has NaN? norm ensures finite.

`_cross_dot(p,a)`: similar threshold maybe first p dense (length2n-1) not sparse. Use direct if n<=512, else FFT.
```
def _cross_dot(p,a):
    n=a.size
    size=len(p)+n-1
    if n <= 512:
       conv=np.convolve(p, a[::-1], 'full')
       return conv[n-1:n-1+n]
    N=1; while N<size: N<<=1
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N); fa=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fp*fa,N)
    return conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
For gradient, tiny negative due FFT? p nonnegative, a nonnegative, h positive; can `np.maximum(h,0)` maybe.

`_beta_obj(u,beta)`:
```
def _beta_obj(u, beta):
   z=u-np.max(u)
   e=np.exp(z)
   a=e/e.sum()
   c=_conv_square(a)
   maxc=float(np.max(c))
   y=beta*(c-maxc)
   ey=np.exp(y)
   s=float(ey.sum())
   if s<=0: s=1.0
   Mbeta=maxc + np.log(s)/beta
   p=ey/s
   h=_cross_dot(p,a)
   g=2.0*h
   grad = g - a * float(np.sum(g))
   return float(Mbeta), grad
```
Potential if beta very large and c has zeros, y zeros minus max negative; exp fine.
`_softmax(u)` used elsewhere.

`_smooth_anneal(a_init,best,rng,time_limit)`:
```
def _smooth_anneal(a_init, best, rng, time_limit):
    a = _norm(a_init)
    start = time.monotonic()
    if a.size < 3 or time_limit <= 0: return a
    c = _conv_square(a); M = float(c.max())
    if not finite or M<=0: return a
    beta = max(1.0, 5.0 / M)
    maxit = 25 if a.size > 1500 else 60
    u = np.log(a + 1e-15)
    u = u - u.max()
    for _ in range(4):
        if time.monotonic() - start > time_limit: break
        # maybe set time per call? minimize cannot; rely maxit
        res = minimize(_beta_obj, u, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', args=(beta,),
                       options={'maxiter': maxit, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-6, 'maxls':50})
        if hasattr(res, 'x') and res.x is not None:
            u = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
        a = _softmax(u)
        # sparseify if already sparse
        maxa = float(a.max())
        small = 1e-13 * maxa
        a[a < small] = 0.0
        a = _norm(a)
        _try_seq(a, best, "smooth")
        # internal for local? no
        beta *= 4.0
        if time.monotonic() - start > time_limit: break
    return a
```

`_block_perturb`:
```
def _block_perturb(a, rng):
   n=a.size
   candidate=a.copy()
   if n<2: return candidate
   wmin=max(1,n//300)
   wmax=max(wmin+1,n//3)
   width=int(rng.integers(wmin,wmax))
   start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
   sigma=0.15 if rng.random()<0.7 else 0.5
   factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma)))
   candidate[start:start+width] *= factor
   if rng.random()<0.15:
       candidate[start:start+width] = 0.0 # zero out? maybe not
   if rng.random()<0.2:
       candidate = 0.5*(candidate + candidate[::-1])
   return np.clip(candidate,0,1000)
```
Maybe zeroing creates new support. After norm.

Add pool function:
```
def _add_pool(pool, vec, max_len=40):
   v = _norm(vec)
   sc = _internal_score(v)
   if not finite: return
   # avoid duplicates: if exists with same shape and np.allclose? skip maybe.
   for ps, pv in pool:
      if pv.size == v.size and np.allclose(v, pv, atol=1e-12): return
   if len(pool) < max_len: pool.append((sc,v.copy()))
   else:
      worst = max(pool, key=lambda item: item[0])
      if sc < worst[0]: pool[pool.index(worst)] = (sc,v.copy())
   return sc
```

Now, let's think about exact evaluator effect with zeros and sc length. If we return a sequence with trailing zeros, score penalized by n. We should trim trailing zeros to reduce n unless zeros inside are part of pattern. We can trim leading/trailing zeros? Leading zeros also increase n and affect convolution? If sequence has leading zeros, can we drop them? Since convolution of shifted sequence: If we remove leading zeros, length decreases and sum same, max convolution? If sequence a with leading zeros, shifting it left by k changes n and convolution? Example a=[0,0,1,1]? Remove leading zeros yields [1,1] length2. Convolution max originally 1 (for two ones) sum2 score=2*4*1/4=2; trimmed length2 max1 sum2 score=2; same? In general dropping leading zeros shifts all indices by k, convolution just shifted, max same, n smaller, sum same => score lower. So always trim leading zeros. Dropping trailing zeros also shifts? If trailing zeros, original a has support ending at e< n-1. Removing trailing zeros reduces length to e+1, convolution max? Original convolution of support up to e has max same as trimmed (since zeros after don't contribute), n smaller => score lower. So any leading/trailing zeros are bad. We should trim leading and trailing zeros from any candidate before evaluating/returning. BUT if zeros inside, keep. Also if we have internal structure with trailing zeros deliberately? Never beneficial because n factor; if max doesn't decrease by trimming (it stays same because zeros don't contribute), score decreases. Thus always trim both ends. Important! Our greedy vectors length last+1 trimmed. But smooth/softmax can introduce tiny positive at ends; zeroing and trimming helpful. For LP updates may create zeros at ends? We should trim.

Implement `_trim_trim?`:
```
def _trim(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).copy()
   if a.size==0: return a
   # remove leading zeros
   nz = np.nonzero(a > 0)[0]
   if nz.size==0: return a
   a = a[nz[0]:nz[-1]+1]
   return a
```
Need tolerance: remove values below 1e-15 maybe before trimming. If trim removes all, fallback.
But if there are tiny positive at ends from numerical, trim not remove unless threshold. We can zero values < 1e-14 * max before trim.
`_clean_sequence(a)`:
```
def _clean_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1)
    if a.size==0: return a
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    mx = float(a.max()) if a.size else 0
    if mx <=0: return np.ones(1)
    a[a < 1e-15 * mx] = 0.0
    nz = np.nonzero(a)[0]
    if nz.size==0: return np.ones(1)
    a = a[nz[0]:nz[-1]+1]
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    return a/s
```
Use in `_try_seq` and final. Trimming changes shape; score improves. Need be careful: If a has a zero inside but trimming only ends; okay. If a has leading tiny values but significant after, trim may shift support; this is valid because dropping leading zeros shifts whole sequence left; the relative pattern same. Does convolution max remain same? If sequence a with leading zeros zero up to k, new a' = a[k:]. Convolution a*a is a'*a' shifted by 2k? Actually (a*a)(m) = (a'*a')(m-2k) for m≥2k; max same. n lower. Good. Trailing similar. So always trim.

This has big impact: If smooth/LP adds mass near ends, trim may remove if zeros; if positive, perhaps should set to zero if they don't help? Trimming only zeros. Could we drop an end with small positive if it reduces score? Maybe we can consider after optimization: For a sequence, removing the first element (if small) changes convolution not just shift. We could try dropping ends while it improves score, even if positive. For example if endpoint weight small but contributes to low peak and increases n? Dropping it reduces n but may change max? Since n factor decreases, likely improves unless max increases. We can implement a postprocessing: while first or last positive, try removing it and clean/trim; if internal_score lower, accept. This can shorten length and improve. However if endpoint is meaningful (e.g., Golomb support starts at0 and ends last positive), removing could increase relative max because mass distribution changes. But we can test.

`_greedy_iterative(R, L, strategy, rng, best, pool, min_score_to_store=2.0)`:
```
def _greedy_iterative(R, L, strategy, rng, best, pool):
   if L<4: return
   counts=np.zeros(2*L-1, dtype=np.int64)
   S=[0]
   counts[0]=1
   m=1
   while m < L:
      last = S[-1]
      start=last+1
      if start >= L: break
      xrange = np.arange(start,L, dtype=np.int64)
      base=int(counts.max())
      cand = np.full(xrange.shape, base, dtype=np.int64)
      for s in S:
          cand = np.maximum(cand, counts[s + xrange] + 2)
      diag = 2*xrange
      # diag valid <=2L-2, yes
      cand = np.maximum(cand, counts[diag] + 1)
 feas = cand <= R
      if not np.any(feas): break
      idxs=np.nonzero(feas)[0]
      idx = choose...
      x=int(xrange[idx])
      for s in S:
         counts[x+s] += 2
      counts[2*x] += 1
      S.append(x); m +=1; last=x
      if m % 4 == 0 or m==2 or last == L-1:
          n=last+1
          maxc=int(counts[:2*n-1].max())
          sc=float(2*n*maxc)/(m*m)
          if sc < 2.02:
             vec=np.zeros(n); vec[S]=1.0/m
             _add_pool(pool, vec, max_pool=50)
             if sc < best[0]: _try_seq(vec,best,f"greedy R{R} m{m}")
```
But note if `last < L-1` and no feasible x <L, the loop breaks; the current support may have span n< L. Good.
For strategy 'min', if L large, sequence may be nested across calls; but fine.
Potential memory/time: In loop, `cand` allocation length L-last. For each s in S, `np.maximum` creates new array; we can use `np.maximum(cand, counts[s+xrange]+2, out=cand)` to reduce allocations? `counts[s+xrange]+2` allocates. Could do:
```
vals = counts[s + xrange] + 2
np.maximum(cand, vals, out=cand)
```
Still vals alloc. Could use `cand = np.maximum(cand, counts[s+xrange] + 2)` okay.
For m up to 100, range thousands, many allocations; okay limited.

Maybe R large and strategy min can add many points quickly until no feasible; for L=4096,R=5, m~200; per addition ~m*range allocating ~200*2000*8*200=640MB memory churn, maybe okay but time. Use max_len 1024.

Let's outline `propose_candidate` more concretely:

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng=...
    t0=time.monotonic()
    budget=max(10,float(budget_s))
    deadline=t0 + budget - 2.0
    best=[np.inf, None]
    # base valid
    for n in [8, 32, 128]: _try_seq(np.ones(n), best, f"uniform{n}")
    # global
    ...
    pool=[]
    _add_pool(pool, best[1])
    # if best inf, fallback?
    # support phase end
    support_deadline = t0 + min(0.40*budget, 900.0) maybe? We need ensure at least
    specs = []
    for R in [2,3,4,5,6]:
       for L in [128,256,512,1024,2048]:
          specs.append((R,L,'min'))
          if R in (3,4,5): specs.append((R,L,'rand'))
    idx=0
    while time.monotonic() < support_deadline and idx < len(specs):
        R,L,strat=specs[idx]; idx+=1
        if L*... maybe skip if time per? 
        try: _greedy_iterative(R,L,strat,rng,best,pool)
        except Exception: pass

def _random_sparse(n,rng):

   m=min(max(2,m), n)

Function top-level.

Implement `_sort_pool(pool,k)`:
```
def _sort_pool(pool, k):
    rem = list(pool)
    out=[]
    while rem and len(out)<k:
       mi=0; ms=rem[0][0]
       for i in range(1,len(rem)):
          if rem[i][0] < ms:
             ms=rem[i][0]; mi=i
       out.append(rem.pop(mi))
    return out
```

def _refine_lp(a_init, best, rng, time_limit):
    a = _clean_sequence(a_init)
    t_end = time.monotonic() + time_limit
    local_score = _internal_score(a)
    _try_seq(a,best,"lp init")
    for it in range(12):
       if time.monotonic() >= t_end: break
       lp_time = min(10.0, max(1.0, (t_end - time.monotonic())/6.0))
       g = _lp_direction(a, lp_time)
       if g is None: break
       g = _clean_sequence(g)
       improved = False
       ts = [1.0, 0.8, 0.6, 0.4, 0.25, 0.15, 0.05]
       # maybe random ts
       for t in ts:
           cand = (1-t)*a + t*g
           cand = _clean_sequence(cand)
           sc = _internal_score(cand)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < local_score - 1e-10:
               local_score = sc; a = cand; improved=True
               _try_seq(cand,best,f"lp t{t}")
               break
       if not improved:
          # try random t
          for _ in range(3):
             t = float(rng.uniform(0.1,1.0))
             cand = (1-t)*a + t*g
             cand=cand...
             sc=...
             if sc<local_score...
          # If no improvement after 2 iterations, stop
       if not improved:
          # maybe accept small worse? no
          if it >=2: break
    # final careful end reduction
    a, local_score = _reduce_ends(a, local_score, max_iter=20)
    _try_seq(a,best,"lp ends")
    return a
```

def _reduce_ends(a, current_score=None, max_iter=30):
    a=_clean_sequence(a)
    if a.size==0: return a, np.inf
    if current_score is None: current_score=_internal_score(a)
    for _ in range(max_iter):
       changed=False
       if a.size>2:
          b=_clean_sequence(a[1:])
          sc=_internal_score(b)
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current_score - 1e-12:
             a=b; current_score=sc; changed=True
       if a.size>2 and changed==False? We can still try right even if left changed? Use for each loop two tries.
       if a.size>2:
          b=_clean_sequence(a[:-1])
          sc=...
       if not changed: break
    return a,current_score
```

Now, `_random_sparse` top-level:
```
def _random_sparse(n, rng):
   n=int(n)
   if n<2: return np.ones(max(2,n))/2
   upper = min(n, max(4, int(2.5 * np.sqrt(n)) + 2))
   lower = max(2, upper//6)
   m=int(rng.integers(lower, upper+1))
   sup=rng.choice(n, m, replace=False)
   sup=np.sort(sup)
   a=np.zeros(n)
   a[sup]=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(m))
   return _clean_sequence(a)
```
This uses np.sqrt.

Now, final search `_finish_random_search(pool,best,rng,deadline)`:
```
def _finish_random_search(pool,best,rng,deadline):
   base = best[1] if best[1] is not None and np.isfinite(best[0]) else np.ones(64)/64
   current = _clean_sequence(base)
   loc = _internal_score(current)
   if not finite or loc >= best[0]:
      # maybe use top pool
   stall=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.5:
      if loc < best[0] - 1e-10:
         _try_seq(current,best,"final")
      # choose action
      r = rng.random()
      if r < 0.85:
          cand = _block_perturb(current, rng)
      elif r < 0.95 and pool:
          sc,pv = pool[int(rng.integers(len(pool)))]
          cand = _block_perturb(pv, rng)
      else:
          n = int(rng.integers(16, 1200))
          if rng.random()<0.5:
             cand = _random_sparse(n, rng)
          else:
             cand = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n))
      cand = _clean_sequence(cand)
      sc = _internal_score(cand)
      if not np.isfinite(sc):
          cand = _clean_sequence(current); sc=loc; stall+=1; continue
      if sc < loc - 1e-10:
          loc = sc; current = cand; stall = 0
      else:
          # accept worse rarely to escape
          if rng.random() < max(0.0, 0.03 - 0.01*stall/100.0):
              loc = sc; current = cand; stall = 0
          else:
              stall += 1
      if stall > 3000:
          # reset to best or random
          if rng.random()<0.5:
             current = _clean_sequence(best[1])
          else:
             current = _random_sparse(int(rng.integers(32,800)), rng)
          loc = _internal_score(current)
          stall=0
      if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.5: break
```

```python
"""
Search algorithm for low peak convolution sequences.

The evaluator is invariant under scaling (for valid positive sums), so we work
with sequences normalized to sum 1 and minimize 2*n*max(conv(a,a)).

The search combines:
  1) Greedy construction of dense B_2[g]-like supports with bounded pair-sum
     multiplicities, including several g values and randomized variants.
  2) Continuous smoothing/minimax refinement with a log-sum-exp approximation
     to the max coefficient, using L-BFGS in softmax parameters.
  3) Linear-programming direction updates inspired by the previous state of
     the art: given f, solve max sum(g) s.t. 0 <= g and f*g <= max(f*f),
     after normalizing f.  The raw LP is scaled for numerical stability.
  4) Local random block perturbations and random restarts in the final phase.

The program always returns the best finite sequence found.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import linprog, minimize
from scipy.sparse import coo_matrix


def _clean_sequence(a):
    """Normalize, zero tiny values, trim leading/trailing zeros."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    mx = float(a.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
    a[a < 1e-16 * mx] = 0.0
    nz = np.nonzero(a)[0]
    if nz.size == 0:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
    a = a[int(nz[0]):int(nz[-1]) + 1]
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
    return a / s


def _internal_score(a):
    """Fast internal evaluator, matching valid inputs of evaluate_sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    c = _conv_square(a)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))


def _safe_score(seq):
    """Call the authoritative evaluator, defensively."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq])
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return np.inf
    return val


def _try_seq(seq, best, tag=""):
    """Normalize/clean a candidate and update best if it improves."""
    a = _clean_sequence(seq)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    sc_in = _internal_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc_in):
        return np.inf

    # For exact comparison, call the evaluator only when the internal score is
    # promising.  This avoids slowing down long candidates that are obviously bad.
    if best[0] == np.inf or sc_in + 1e-7 < best[0]:
        sc = _safe_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best[0]:
            best[0] = float(sc)
            best[1] = a.copy()
            print("update %s score=%.8f len=%d support=%d" %
                  (tag, best[0], a.size, int(np.count_nonzero(a))))
            return float(sc)
    return sc_in


def _softmax(u):
    u = np.asarray(u, dtype=np.float64)
    z = u - float(np.max(u))
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(u.size, dtype=np.float64) / u.size
    return e / s


def _conv_square(a):
    """
    Square convolution a*a.  Uses sparse loops when support is small,
    direct convolution for moderate dense vectors, FFT for large dense vectors.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    mx = max(1.0, float(a.max()))
    sup = np.nonzero(a > 1e-16 * mx)[0]
    if sup.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)

    # Sparse fast path: total work O(|sup|^2), acceptable for small supports.
    if sup.size <= 1000 and sup.size * sup.size <= 1_000_000:
        c = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
        vals = a[sup]
        for i in sup:
            c[i + sup] += a[i] * vals
        return np.maximum(c, 0.0)

    if n <= 2048:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
        return np.maximum(c, 0.0)

    N = 1
    while N < size:
        N <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:size]
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def _cross_dot_pv(p, a):
    """
    h_i = sum_j a_j * p_{i+j}, for i=0..n-1.
    This is the derivative shape for c_k = sum_{i+j=k} a_i a_j.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 512:
        conv = np.convolve(p, a[::-1], "full")
        return conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]
    size = len(p) + n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < size:
        N <<= 1
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], N)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fp * fa, N)
    return conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]


def _beta_objective(u, beta):
    """
    Smooth approximation to max_k c_k with softmax-sum constraint.
    Returns value and gradient w.r.t. u.
    """
    a = _softmax(u)
    c = _conv_square(a)
    maxc = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(maxc):
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(u)

    y = beta * (c - maxc)
    ey = np.exp(y)
    s = float(ey.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        s = 1.0
    Mbeta = maxc + np.log(s) / beta
    p = ey / s
    h = _cross_dot_pv(p, a)
    g = 2.0 * h
    grad = g - a * float(np.sum(g))
    if not np.isfinite(Mbeta):
        Mbeta = 1e100
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad = np.zeros_like(u)
    return float(Mbeta), grad


def _block_perturb(a, rng):
    """Random multiplicative block perturbation, with occasional symmetrization."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1).copy()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return a
    wmin = max(1, n // 300)
    wmax = max(wmin + 1, min(n, n // 3 + 1))
    width = int(rng.integers(wmin, wmax))
    start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
    sigma = 0.12 if rng.random() < 0.7 else 0.35
    factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
    a[start:start + width] *= factor
    if rng.random() < 0.12:
        a[start:start + width] = 0.0
    if rng.random() < 0.18:
        a = 0.5 * (a + a[::-1])
    return np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)


def _random_sparse(n, rng):
    """Random sparse Dirichlet support."""
    n = int(n)
    if n < 2:
        return np.ones(max(2, n), dtype=np.float64) / max(2, n)
    upper = min(n, max(4, int(2.5 * np.sqrt(n)) + 2))
    lower = max(2, upper // 6)
    if lower >= upper:
        m = max(2, min(n, upper))
    else:
        m = int(rng.integers(lower, upper + 1))
    sup = rng.choice(n, m, replace=False)
    sup = np.sort(sup)
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[sup] = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64))
    return _clean_sequence(a)


def _add_pool(pool, vec, max_pool=40):
    """Maintain a small pool of good candidates."""
    a = _clean_sequence(vec)
    sc = _internal_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return sc

    for ps, pv in pool:
        if pv.size != a.size:
            continue
        if pv.size <= 6000:
            if np.allclose(a, pv, rtol=0.0, atol=1e-12):
                return ps
        else:
            if (np.allclose(a[:200], pv[:200], rtol=0.0, atol=1e-12) and
                    np.allclose(a[-200:], pv[-200:], rtol=0.0, atol=1e-12)):
                return ps

    if len(pool) < max_pool:
        pool.append((sc, a.copy()))
    else:
        wi = 0
        ws = -np.inf
        for i, item in enumerate(pool):
            if item[0] > ws:
                ws = item[0]
                wi = i
        if sc < ws:
            pool[wi] = (sc, a.copy())
    return sc


def _sort_pool(pool, k):
    """Return top-k pool items sorted by score, without lambda functions."""
    rem = list(pool)
    out = []
    while rem and len(out) < k:
        mi = 0
        ms = rem[0][0]
        for i in range(1, len(rem)):
            if rem[i][0] < ms:
                ms = rem[i][0]
                mi = i
        out.append(rem.pop(mi))
    return out


def _greedy_iterative(R, L, strategy, rng, best, pool):
    """
    Greedy construction of a set S of distinct positions with pair-sum counts
    at most R.  Evaluate prefixes as supports with equal weights.
    """
    if L < 4:
        return
    L = int(L)
    R = int(R)
    counts = np.zeros(2 * L - 1, dtype=np.int64)
    S = [0]
    counts[0] = 1
    m = 1

    while m < L:
        last = S[-1]
        start = last + 1
        if start >= L:
            break
        xrange = np.arange(start, L, dtype=np.int64)
        base = int(counts.max())
        cand = np.full(xrange.shape, base, dtype=np.int64)

        for s in S:
            np.maximum(cand, counts[s + xrange] + 2, out=cand)
        np.maximum(cand, counts[2 * xrange] + 1, out=cand)

        feas = cand <= R
        if not np.any(feas):
            break
        idxs = np.nonzero(feas)[0]

        if strategy == "min":
            idx = idxs[0]
        elif strategy == "max":
            idx = int(idxs[-1])
        elif strategy == "rand":
            w = (float(R) + 1.0 - float(cand[idxs]))
            s = float(w.sum())
            if s <= 0.0:
                idx = idxs[0]
            else:
                p = w / s
                idx = int(idxs[int(rng.choice(len(idxs), p=p))])
        else:
            bestc = int(cand[idxs].min())
            min_idxs = idxs[cand[idxs] == bestc]
            idx = int(min_idxs[len(min_idxs) // 2])

        x = int(xrange[int(idx)])

        for s in S:
            counts[x + s] += 2
        counts[2 * x] += 1
        S.append(x)
        m += 1
        last = x

        if m == 2 or m % 4 == 0 or last == L - 1:
            n = last + 1
            maxc = int(counts[:2 * n - 1].max())
            sc = float(2 * n * maxc) / float(m * m)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < 2.03:
                vec = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                vec[S] = 1.0 / float(m)
                _add_pool(pool, vec, max_pool=48)
                if best[0] == np.inf or sc + 1e-8 < best[0]:
                    _try_seq(vec, best, "greedy R%d m%d" % (R, m))

        if 2 * last >= 2 * L - 1:
            break


def _lp_direction(f, lp_time=5.0, max_nz=3_000_000):
    """
    LP direction: maximize sum(g) subject to f*g <= max(f*f), g >= 0.
    For numerical stability, set F=f/M and solve F*g <= 1.
    """
    f = _clean_sequence(f)
    n = int(f.size)
    if n < 2:
        return None
    M = float(np.max(_conv_square(f)))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return None
    F = f / M
    sup = np.nonzero(F > 1e-14)[0]
    if sup.size == 0:
        return None
    if sup.size * n > max_nz:
        return None

    pos = np.repeat(sup.astype(np.int64, copy=False), n)
    cols = np.tile(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64), sup.size)
    rows = pos + cols
    data = np.repeat(F[sup], n)

    shape = (2 * n - 1, n)
    try:
        B = coo_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=shape).tocsr()
    except Exception:
        return None

    c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    ub = 10000.0
    bounds = [(0.0, ub)] * n

    try:
        res = linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=B,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"time_limit": max(0.1, float(lp_time)), "disp": False}
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    x = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if x is None:
        return None
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size != n:
        return None
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, None)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return None
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    g = x / s
    g = _clean_sequence(g)
    return g


def _reduce_ends(a, current_score=None, max_iter=30):
    """Remove leading/trailing elements while they visibly improve score."""
    a = _clean_sequence(a)
    if current_score is None:
        current_score = _internal_score(a)
    for _ in range(max_iter):
        changed = False
        if a.size > 1:
            b = _clean_sequence(a[1:])
            sc = _internal_score(b)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc + 1e-12 < current_score:
                a = b
                current_score = sc
                changed = True
        if a.size > 1:
            b = _clean_sequence(a[:-1])
            sc = _internal_score(b)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc + 1e-12 < current_score:
                a = b
                current_score = sc
                changed = True
        if not changed:
            break
    return a, current_score


def _smooth_anneal(a_init, best, rng, time_limit):
    """Local continuous refinement using log-sum-exp approximation."""
    a = _clean_sequence(a_init)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    if a.size < 3 or time_limit <= 0.0:
        return a

    M = float(np.max(_conv_square(a)))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return a

    beta = max(1.0, 5.0 / max(M, 1e-15))
    n = int(a.size)
    maxit = 25 if n > 1500 else (45 if n > 500 else 80)
    u = np.log(a + 1e-15)
    u = u - float(np.max(u))

    for _rep in range(2):
        for _beta_step in range(3):
            if time.monotonic() - t0 > time_limit:
                return a
            try:
                res = minimize(
                    _beta_objective,
                    u,
                    jac=True,
                    method="L-BFGS-B",
                    args=(beta,),
                    options={"maxiter": maxit, "maxls": 50,
                             "ftol": 1e-12, "gtol": 1e-6}
                )
            except Exception:
                break

            if res.x is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
                u = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            a = _softmax(u)
            a = _clean_sequence(a)

            _try_seq(a, best, "smooth")

            # Occasional tiny random kick to escape very flat valleys.
            if rng.random() < 0.25:
                u = u + rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, size=n)

            beta *= 4.0
            if beta > 1e6:
                break
        if time.monotonic() - t0 > time_limit:
            break

    return a


def _refine_lp(a_init, best, rng, time_limit):
    """Iterate LP direction updates, accepting only internal improvements."""
    a = _clean_sequence(a_init)
    t_end = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(time_limit))
    local_score = _internal_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(local_score):
        return a

    _try_seq(a, best, "lp-init")

    no_improve = 0
    for it in range(10):
        if time.monotonic() >= t_end:
            break
        remaining = max(0.5, t_end - time.monotonic())
        lp_time = min(10.0, max(0.8, remaining / 6.0))

        g = _lp_direction(a, lp_time=lp_time)
        if g is None:
            break

        improved = False
        ts = (1.0, 0.8, 0.6, 0.4, 0.25, 0.15, 0.05)
        for t in ts:
            cand = (1.0 - t) * a + t * g
            cand = _clean_sequence(cand)
            sc = _internal_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc + 1e-10 < local_score:
                local_score = sc
                a = cand
                improved = True
                no_improve = 0
                _try_seq(cand, best, "lp-t%.2f-i%d" % (t, it))
                break

        if not improved:
            # A few random t values, in case deterministic grid is bad.
            for _r in range(4):
                t = float(rng.uniform(0.08, 1.0))
                cand = (1.0 - t) * a + t * g
                cand = _clean_sequence(cand)
                sc = _internal_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc + 1e-10 < local_score:
                    local_score = sc
                    a = cand
                    improved = True
                    no_improve = 0
                    _try_seq(cand, best, "lp-trand-i%d" % it)
                    break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve >= 2:
                break

    a, local_score = _reduce_ends(a, local_score, max_iter=10)
    _try_seq(a, best, "lp-reduce")
    return a


def _finish_random_search(pool, best, rng, deadline):
    """Final random local search/meta-restart phase."""
    base = best[1] if best[1] is not None else np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
    current = _clean_sequence(base)
    loc = _internal_score(current)
    if not np.isfinite(loc):
        current = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
        loc = _internal_score(current)

    stall = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.75:
        if loc + 1e-10 < best[0]:
            _try_seq(current, best, "final")

        r = float(rng.random())
        if r < 0.84:
            cand = _block_perturb(current, rng)
        elif r < 0.94 and len(pool) > 0:
            base_i = int(rng.integers(0, len(pool)))
            cand = _block_perturb(pool[base_i][1], rng)
        else:
            n = int(rng.integers(16, 1200))
            if rng.random() < 0.55:
                cand = _random_sparse(n, rng)
            else:
                cand = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

        cand = _clean_sequence(cand)
        sc = _internal_score(cand)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            stall += 1
            cand = current
            sc = loc

        if sc + 1e-10 < loc:
            loc = sc
            current = cand
            stall = 0
        else:
            # Very mild Metropolis acceptance to escape basins.
            accept = 0.02
            if rng.random() < accept and sc < loc * 1.35 + 0.3:
                loc = sc
                current = cand
                stall = 0
            else:
                stall += 1

        if stall > 2500:
            if rng.random() < 0.45:
                current = _clean_sequence(best[1])
            else:
                current = _random_sparse(int(rng.integers(32, 900)), rng)
            loc = _internal_score(current)
            stall = 0
        # Do not exceed deadline with a very long vector.
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.75:
            break


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Return a sequence of nonnegative heights minimizing the objective."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(10.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + budget - 2.0

    best = [np.inf, None]

    # Basic valid fallbacks and useful small grids.
    for n in (4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128):
        _try_seq(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), best, "uniform%d" % n)

    # If a previously found sequence is available, use it and a few windows.
    try:
        ga = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if ga.size > 0:
            if ga.size <= 20000:
                _try_seq(ga, best, "global-full")
            for L in (64, 128, 256, 512):
                if ga.size >= L:
                    for off in (0, ga.size // 3, ga.size // 2):
                        off = max(0, min(off, ga.size - L))
                        _try_seq(ga[off:off + L], best, "global-win%d" % L)
    except Exception:
        pass

    if best[1] is None:
        best[1] = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
        best[0] = _safe_score(best[1])

    pool = []
    _add_pool(pool, best[1])

    # ---------------- Phase 1: combinatorial support construction ----------
    support_deadline = t0 + max(20.0, min(0.25 * budget, 500.0))

    specs = []
    for R in (2, 3, 4, 5, 6):
        for L in (128, 256, 512, 1024):
            specs.append((R, L, "min"))
            if R in (3, 4, 5):
                specs.append((R, L, "rand"))
    for R in (3, 4, 5):
        specs.append((R, 2048, "min"))

    sp_idx = 0
    while sp_idx < len(specs) and time.monotonic() < support_deadline:
        R, L, strat = specs[sp_idx]
        sp_idx += 1
        try:
            _greedy_iterative(R, L, strat, rng, best, pool)
        except Exception:
            pass

    _add_pool(pool, best[1])
    for n in (32, 64, 128, 256, 512):
        _add_pool(pool, rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))
        _add_pool(pool, _random_sparse(n, rng))

    # ---------------- Phase 2: smooth local minimization --------------------
    smooth_deadline = t0 + max(60.0, min(0.60 * budget, 1200.0))
    top = _sort_pool(pool, 30)
    idx = 0
    for item in top:
        rem = smooth_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 8.0:
            break
        allow = min(90.0, max(3.0, rem / float(max(1, len(top) - idx))))
        try:
            a2 = _smooth_anneal(item[1], best, rng, allow)
            _add_pool(pool, a2)
        except Exception:
            pass
        idx += 1

    # Smooth a couple of fresh random starts, even if not in top pool.
    for n in (64, 256, 1024):
        rem = smooth_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 15.0:
            break
        allow = min(45.0, max(5.0, rem / 3.0))
        try:
            if rng.random() < 0.5:
                a0 = _random_sparse(n, rng)
            else:
                a0 = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
            a2 = _smooth_anneal(a0, best, rng, allow)
            _add_pool(pool, a2)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Add a few products of top candidates, if short enough.
    top = _sort_pool(pool, 5)
    for i in range(min(3, len(top))):
        for j in range(min(3, len(top))):
            if time.monotonic() > smooth_deadline:
                break
            A = top[i][1]
            B = top[j][1]
            if A.size + B.size - 1 <= 5000:
                try:
                    C = np.convolve(A, B)
                    _add_pool(pool, C)
                except Exception:
                    pass
        if time.monotonic() > smooth_deadline:
            break

    # ---------------- Phase 3: LP direction refinement ----------------------
    lp_deadline = t0 + max(120.0, min(0.83 * budget, 1950.0))
    top = _sort_pool(pool, 25)
    idx = 0
    for item in top:
        rem = lp_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 12.0:
            break
        a0 = item[1]
        if a0.size > 8000:
            continue
        allow = min(120.0, max(5.0, rem / max(1, 5)))
        try:
            a2 = _refine_lp(a0, best, rng, allow)
            _add_pool(pool, a2)
        except Exception:
            pass
        idx += 1

    # ---------------- Phase 4: final search and cleanup ---------------------
    top = _sort_pool(pool, 10)
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
        for item in top[:3]:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0
            if rem < 20.0:
                break
            allow = min(90.0, max(10.0, rem / 3.0))
            try:
                a2 = _refine_lp(item[1], best, rng, allow)
                _add_pool(pool, a2)
            except Exception:
                pass

    _finish_random_search(pool, best, rng, deadline)

    # Carefully reduce the final sequence's endpoints, and try one more
    # short local polish if time remains.
    if best[1] is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
        try:
            red, _ = _reduce_ends(best[1], None, max_iter=80)
            _try_seq(red, best, "final-end-reduce")
        except Exception:
            pass

    if best[1] is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 3.0:
        try:
            _smooth_anneal(best[1], best, rng, max(2.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 1.0))
        except Exception:
            pass

    seq = best[1] if best[1] is not None else np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
    seq = _clean_sequence(seq)
    final_score = _safe_score(seq)
    if np.isfinite(final_score):
        best[0] = float(min(best[0], final_score))
        try:
            if final_score < best[0] + 1e-12:
                seq = _cain_sequence if False else seq  # no-op safeguard
        except Exception:
            pass

    print("final best score=%.8f len=%d support=%d" %
          (best[0], seq.size, int(np.count_nonzero(seq))))
    return [float(x) for x in seq]
```
